way out

00:09:57 --> 00:10:02
uh where they take your guns away we

00:09:59 --> 00:10:04
still got a uniform still male dominated

00:10:01 --> 00:10:05
uh still combative and then eventually

00:10:03 --> 00:10:07
ou're just a normal citizen

00:10:04 --> 00:10:09
and so i think that in some weird way i

00:10:06 --> 00:10:09
was you know very fortunate to kind of

00:10:08 --> 00:10:11
on the

00:10:08 --> 00:10:12
ntry and exit have that experience

00:10:10 --> 00:10:16
where other guys didn't

00:10:12 --> 00:10:19
so what you were deployed to iraq

00:10:15 --> 00:10:20
were there any memories experiences that

00:10:18 --> 00:10:23
changed you

00:10:19 --> 00:10:25
or in general like you're probably a

00:10:22 --> 00:10:27
different man on the other side of it

00:10:24 --> 00:10:28
how did that time change you i think

00:10:26 --> 00:10:30
there was two main

00:10:27 --> 00:10:32
takeaways that i took right so one was

00:10:29 --> 00:10:33
um not a specific moment but on multiple

00:10:31 --> 00:10:35
occasions

00:10:32 --> 00:10:37
i remember we were driving down the road

00:10:34 --> 00:10:40
and i would look and there would be a

00:10:36 --> 00:10:41
10 12 14 year old kid and if you've

00:10:39 --> 00:10:44
never seen somebody who

00:10:40 --> 00:10:45
literally has hate and disdain in their

00:10:43 --> 00:10:47
eyes towards you

00:10:44 --> 00:10:49
just not necessarily to you personally

00:10:46 --> 00:10:51
just to the uniform to what you stand

00:10:48 --> 00:10:52
for it's all the stuff

00:10:50 --> 00:10:54
when you see it you see it right and i

00:10:51 --> 00:10:56
always think of um

00:10:53 --> 00:10:57
if you've ever uh watched the movie a 12

00:10:55 --> 00:10:59
strong

00:10:56 --> 00:11:01
and in it they've got this afghan

00:10:58 --> 00:11:02
istan warlord and he's

00:11:00 --> 00:11:04
talking to a bunch of soldiers and he

00:11:02 --> 00:11:06
says you know you don't have killer eyes

00:11:03 --> 00:11:07
you do you do and he's just like he can

00:11:05 --> 00:11:08
just tell right he's seen so many

00:11:06 --> 00:11:11
soldiers

00:11:07 --> 00:11:13
and so i think that that was a a memory

00:11:10 --> 00:11:14
on a number of occasions where i just

00:11:12 --> 00:11:17
saw a young kid

00:11:13 --> 00:11:18
and i just said they hate us right and

00:11:16 --> 00:11:20
it's not

00:11:17 --> 00:11:22
you personally it's what you stand for

00:11:19 --> 00:11:24
but the the really big event was

00:11:21 --> 00:11:26
when you first get to these combat zones

00:11:23 --> 00:11:28
a lot of times what will happen is

00:11:25 --> 00:11:29
you basically your unit teams up with a

00:11:28 --> 00:11:31
unit that's leaving

00:11:28 --> 00:11:32
and there's a handoff and it happens

00:11:30 --> 00:11:33
over a couple of weeks and so you can

00:11:31 --> 00:11:34
imagine almost

00:11:32 --> 00:11:37
you know one percent of our team goes

00:11:33 --> 00:11:39
out with 99 of their team then 5

00:11:36 --> 00:11:40
95 10 90 all the way till it's 50 50 and

00:11:38 --> 00:11:42
then eventually it rotates and then it's

00:11:40 --> 00:11:44
majority of our team and

00:11:41 --> 00:11:46
a small number of their team and so what

00:11:43 --> 00:11:48
a lot of people will explain is the two

00:11:45 --> 00:11:49
most dangerous times in war

00:11:47 --> 00:11:51
e actually the first two or three

00:11:48 --> 00:11:52
weeks in the last two or three weeks

00:11:50 --> 00:11:54
when you first get there you don't know

00:11:52 --> 00:11:55
ho the people are you don't know the

00:11:53 --> 00:11:57
terrain you don't know

00:11:54 --> 00:11:59
you know kind of the local cultures and

00:11:56 --> 00:12:00
who to look out for what signs all that

00:11:58 --> 00:12:01
kind of stuff

00:11:59 --> 00:12:03
and the last two or three weeks is you

00:12:00 --> 00:12:04
basically think you've survived the

00:12:02 --> 00:12:05
deployment you're looking forward to

00:12:03 --> 00:12:06
going home so you become complacent

00:12:04 --> 00:12:08
hings like that

00:12:05 --> 00:12:09
and so we made it through that

00:12:07 --> 00:12:12
ransition period with no issues

00:12:08 --> 00:12:13
uh but the literally the very first

00:12:11 --> 00:12:16
mission uh that we went out

00:12:12 --> 00:12:18
as a team ourselves 100 um

00:12:15 --> 00:12:20
there was two separate incidents uh one

00:12:17 --> 00:12:23
was we were driving in milanite

00:12:19 --> 00:12:23
and uh what we think was a sniper shot

00:12:22 --> 00:12:25
at

00:12:22 --> 00:12:27
a truck that i was in i was standing up

00:12:24 --> 00:12:29
outside of it along with a

00:12:26 --> 00:12:31
nother guy and that was kind of just a

00:12:28 --> 00:12:33
wake-up call again of

00:12:30 --> 00:12:34
never been shot up before right and so

00:12:32 --> 00:12:36
i'll never forget

00:12:33 --> 00:12:39
uh you kind of heard this whiz go by and

00:12:36 --> 00:12:40
the guy next to me was from rural

00:12:38 --> 00:12:41
pennsylvania and he just said

00:12:39 --> 00:12:42
you know look i've never been shot at

00:12:40 --> 00:12:45
before either but i've done a lot of

00:12:41 --> 00:12:48
hunting get the fuck down

00:12:44 --> 00:12:49
okay and so uh we kept driving that

00:12:47 --> 00:12:52
night

00:12:48 --> 00:12:55
um and uh later on in the night uh an

00:12:51 --> 00:12:57
ied went off a guy ran over an ied

00:12:54 --> 00:12:59
uh who was at the front of the convoy

00:12:56 --> 00:13:01
and when that ied went off

00:12:58 --> 00:13:03
uh again i had never been in a convoy

00:13:00 --> 00:13:04
that had been blown up before but the

00:13:02 --> 00:13:05
training kicks in

00:13:03 --> 00:13:07
and so immediately every single person

00:13:04 --> 00:13:08
starts screaming out ied ied and they

00:13:06 --> 00:13:10
start looking and trying to figure it

00:13:07 --> 00:13:11
out and you realize

00:13:09 --> 00:13:14
the united states military did a

00:13:10 --> 00:13:16
fantastic job training us before we went

00:13:13 --> 00:13:17
because in that moment nobody thought

00:13:15 --> 00:13:19
about anything you just did what you had

00:13:16 --> 00:13:21
been programmed to do

00:13:18 --> 00:13:23
and so ultimately kind of through the

00:13:20 --> 00:13:24
nd of that event uh there was u.s

00:13:22 --> 00:13:25
oldier that ended up dying he was uh

00:13:23 --> 00:13:29
shot in the head

00:13:24 --> 00:13:31
and um when we got back to the base uh

00:13:28 --> 00:13:34
kind of after that entire event

00:13:30 --> 00:13:35
i think it just hit us we are at war and

00:13:33 --> 00:13:38
if we make mistakes here

00:13:34 --> 00:13:39
that is the cost of you know kind of

00:13:37 --> 00:13:41
those mistakes

00:13:38 --> 00:13:43
and this was somebody who i didn't know

00:13:40 --> 00:13:43
uh it was somebody who literally showed

00:13:42 --> 00:13:46
up

00:13:42 --> 00:13:47
kind of as secondary support for our

00:13:45 --> 00:13:49
unit

00:13:46 --> 00:13:51
and basically uh was there to help us

00:13:48 --> 00:13:52
right and so it was one of those weird

00:13:50 --> 00:13:54
situations where

00:13:52 --> 00:13:56
uh you have this emotional connection

00:13:53 --> 00:13:58
because it's somebody who

00:13:55 --> 00:13:59
uh shared an event with you but you

00:13:57 --> 00:14:01
didn't know them

00:13:58 --> 00:14:02
right and you learn their name later and

00:14:00 --> 00:14:03
you understand that they have a family

00:14:01 --> 00:14:04
and they have young kids and all this

00:14:02 --> 00:14:06
tuff

00:14:03 --> 00:14:07
and so again as a 20 year old kid you're

00:14:05 --> 00:14:08
kind of processing all this and you just

00:14:06 --> 00:14:11
realize like

00:14:08 --> 00:14:12
the gun i have in my arm my hands it's

00:14:10 --> 00:14:15
real for a reason

00:14:11 --> 00:14:17
right and we better take this seriously

00:14:14 --> 00:14:19
this is not you know us joking around

00:14:16 --> 00:14:20
in kind of the barracks and you know all

00:14:18 --> 00:14:22
the things that you would expect

00:14:19 --> 00:14:23
you know guys are doing the army so i

00:14:21 --> 00:14:24
think that was like the moment where i

00:14:22 --> 00:14:26
said

00:14:23 --> 00:14:27
hey i'm going to learn a lot here uh and

00:14:25 --> 00:14:29
i got to make sure i get home

00:14:26 --> 00:14:31
and you snapping into the training but

00:14:28 --> 00:14:33
nevertheless i mean you're somebody who

00:14:30 --> 00:14:33
thinks philosophically about this world

00:14:32 --> 00:14:36
now

00:14:32 --> 00:14:37
right you're uh very intelligent and

00:14:35 --> 00:14:40
thinks deeply about

00:14:36 --> 00:14:43
he world so looking back you mentioned

00:14:39 --> 00:14:45
hatred in the 14 year old kids eyes

00:14:42 --> 00:14:47
there's death so the way you kind of

00:14:44 --> 00:14:50
describe this whole story

00:14:46 --> 00:14:51
is like training kicks in this this shit

00:14:49 --> 00:14:54
is serious

00:14:50 --> 00:14:55
like this is uh you know there's a

00:14:53 --> 00:14:57
reason there's a gun

00:14:54 --> 00:14:59
in your hand so there's a strategic

00:14:56 --> 00:15:01
element there's like you have to get the

00:14:58 --> 00:15:03
job done there's a task at hand

00:15:00 --> 00:15:05
but at the same time if you zoom out

00:15:02 --> 00:15:08
here's a kid

00:15:04 --> 00:15:10
who has hate for you some of those kids

00:15:07 --> 00:15:13
would probably if they could

00:15:09 --> 00:15:14
would kill you for the thing you stand

00:15:12 --> 00:15:18
for in the uniform

00:15:13 --> 00:15:20
and then there's bullets flying at you

00:15:17 --> 00:15:21
and then there's people that some of

00:15:20 --> 00:15:24
them

00:15:20 --> 00:15:25
you might already care for deeply are

00:15:23 --> 00:15:28
dying

00:15:24 --> 00:15:30
what the hell do you make of that

00:15:27 --> 00:15:32
do you think about that does any of that

00:15:29 --> 00:15:33
haunt you

00:15:31 --> 00:15:35
how do you think about the world having

00:15:32 --> 00:15:37
witnessed that

00:15:34 --> 00:15:39
yeah a lot of times when we would talk

00:15:36 --> 00:15:41
about it we were there

00:15:38 --> 00:15:42
if i was that kid i would want to kill

00:15:40 --> 00:15:44
that person too

00:15:41 --> 00:15:46
right i would have hatred as well right

00:15:43 --> 00:15:48
imagine if in the united states

00:15:45 --> 00:15:50
tomorrow you and i woke up and there was

00:15:47 --> 00:15:51
tanks rolling down the street from

00:15:49 --> 00:15:53
another country

00:15:50 --> 00:15:55
eah and they were basically imposing

00:15:52 --> 00:15:56
their rules on us

00:15:54 --> 00:15:58
whether they thought it was the right

00:15:55 --> 00:15:59
hing to do or not the soldiers were

00:15:57 --> 00:16:01
there they were doing this

00:15:58 --> 00:16:03
we would probably feel not so great

00:16:00 --> 00:16:05
about it right at kind of a minimum

00:16:02 --> 00:16:06
and at a maximum we'd be really really

00:16:04 --> 00:16:08
pissed off we would fight back

00:16:05 --> 00:16:10
and so what you don't understand when

00:16:07 --> 00:16:12
you're in the heat of the moment is

00:16:09 --> 00:16:14
why does this person feel this way and

00:16:11 --> 00:16:17
so what's very weird

00:16:13 --> 00:16:18
is well what happens if a year ago u.s

00:16:16 --> 00:16:21
oldiers came through

00:16:18 --> 00:16:23
they got shot at they returned fire and

00:16:20 --> 00:16:25
they killed that kid's uncle

00:16:22 --> 00:16:27
you'd be pissed off right and so you

00:16:24 --> 00:16:29
just start to understand like

00:16:26 --> 00:16:30
we look at war very black and white we

00:16:28 --> 00:16:32
look at it very much

00:16:29 --> 00:16:34
from a clinical perspective we're going

00:16:31 --> 00:16:35
to go we're going to go kind of invade

00:16:33 --> 00:16:37
somewhere

00:16:34 --> 00:16:39
we are the most dominant military in the

00:16:36 --> 00:16:42
world we're very good at invading

00:16:38 --> 00:16:43
uh and we will crush wherever we invade

00:16:41 --> 00:16:45
but when you're actually on the ground

00:16:42 --> 00:16:45
what you understand is the humanity of

00:16:44 --> 00:16:47
it all

00:16:44 --> 00:16:49
right and so what becomes very

00:16:46 --> 00:16:50
interesting is uh

00:16:48 --> 00:16:52
pretty much every veteran i know that

00:16:49 --> 00:16:53
comes back they're some of the largest

00:16:51 --> 00:16:55
pacifists in the world

00:16:52 --> 00:16:57
and i always revert back to uh marcus

00:16:54 --> 00:16:59
latrell uh who's a famous navy seal

00:16:56 --> 00:17:02
um and there's a movie made about him

00:16:58 --> 00:17:02
and his story uh he gave a speech one

00:17:01 --> 00:17:05
time

00:17:01 --> 00:17:06
that i saw and he basically said listen

00:17:04 --> 00:17:08
if you're a politician your job is to

00:17:05 --> 00:17:10
be the diplomat do everything you

00:17:07 --> 00:17:11
possibly can not to send me and my

00:17:09 --> 00:17:12
friends

00:17:10 --> 00:17:14
anywhere because when you send us we're

00:17:11 --> 00:17:16
gonna bring hell with us

00:17:13 --> 00:17:18
right and understand that is the

00:17:15 --> 00:17:20
business that we are in

00:17:17 --> 00:17:22
but every single other person up until

00:17:19 --> 00:17:23
we get sent has a job to do to prevent

00:17:21 --> 00:17:24
having to send us

00:17:22 --> 00:17:26
and i think that ultimately that's where

00:17:23 --> 00:17:28
you see a lot of kind of this generation

00:17:25 --> 00:17:31
that is fought in iraq and afghanistan

00:17:27 --> 00:17:32
that says listen maybe we shouldn't be

00:17:30 --> 00:17:33
running around the world being the

00:17:31 --> 00:17:35
police

00:17:32 --> 00:17:36
maybe we shouldn't be going and invading

00:17:34 --> 00:17:38
all these different countries

00:17:35 --> 00:17:40
because when you actually get to see

00:17:37 --> 00:17:41
firsthand what happens

00:17:39 --> 00:17:44
it's just something that we should avoid

00:17:40 --> 00:17:46
at all cost but if we have to go

00:17:43 --> 00:17:47
r we have to actually send soldiers

00:17:45 --> 00:17:49
omewhere

00:17:46 --> 00:17:50
understand what happens when that occurs

00:17:48 --> 00:17:52
and you know the united states is the

00:17:50 --> 00:17:55
best in the world of doing it

00:17:51 --> 00:17:57
do you think i'm thinking about that kid

00:17:54 --> 00:17:58
with a hatred do you think there will

00:17:56 --> 00:18:01
always be hatred in the world

00:17:57 --> 00:18:03
o you think from another perspective do

00:18:00 --> 00:18:05
you think there'll be war

00:18:02 --> 00:18:06
always is that a fundamental aspect of

00:18:04 --> 00:18:09
human nature

00:18:05 --> 00:18:10
or is that something we can escape yeah

00:18:08 --> 00:18:11
so war

00:18:09 --> 00:18:13
i think has like very negative

00:18:10 --> 00:18:14
connotations in terms of bullets and

00:18:12 --> 00:18:18
bombs and death

00:18:13 --> 00:18:21
and kind of just very morbid type um

00:18:17 --> 00:18:23
understanding conflict on the other hand

00:18:20 --> 00:18:24
i think people look at and say of course

00:18:22 --> 00:18:25
there'll always be conflict right you

00:18:23 --> 00:18:27
just can't have

00:18:24 --> 00:18:29
billions of people all on the same

00:18:26 --> 00:18:30
planet without some level of

00:18:28 --> 00:18:31
disagreement whether that's a

00:18:29 --> 00:18:33
disagreement of ideas

00:18:30 --> 00:18:35
disagreement over physical you know

00:18:32 --> 00:18:36
geography or something else and so i

00:18:34 --> 00:18:40
think conflict will always

00:18:36 --> 00:18:41
exist the question is what form does war

00:18:39 --> 00:18:43
take moving forward

00:18:40 --> 00:18:45
and so in my mind you know it's starting

00:18:42 --> 00:18:47
to look a lot more clinical

00:18:44 --> 00:18:48
right a lot more drones a lot less

00:18:46 --> 00:18:50
oldiers on the ground

00:18:48 --> 00:18:52
a lot more use of special forces and

00:18:49 --> 00:18:54
kind of these small highly specialized

00:18:51 --> 00:18:54
teams rather than kind of big mechanical

00:18:53 --> 00:18:56
armies

00:18:54 --> 00:18:58
um and then you get into like the

00:18:55 --> 00:19:00
information warfare and kind of cyber

00:18:57 --> 00:19:02
warfare and you start to understand that

00:18:59 --> 00:19:04
we're at war with a lot of people right

00:19:01 --> 00:19:06
now right doesn't mean we're necessarily

00:19:03 --> 00:19:07
dropping bombs uh on their countries

00:19:05 --> 00:19:09
it doesn't necessarily mean we're

00:19:06 --> 00:19:11
sending soldiers there but on a daily

00:19:08 --> 00:19:14
basis we are engaged in these kind of

00:19:10 --> 00:19:16
you know cyber battlefields and so if

00:19:13 --> 00:19:18
that's where war starts to play out

00:19:15 --> 00:19:20
um it one changes the tools and tactics

00:19:17 --> 00:19:21
and techniques that

00:19:19 --> 00:19:23
we need to arm our country and other

00:19:20 --> 00:19:25
countries will arm themselves with

00:19:22 --> 00:19:26
but it also changes the way that we

00:19:24 --> 00:19:29
think about war right

00:19:25 --> 00:19:30
it see it sounds a lot less uh worrisome

00:19:28 --> 00:19:32
if i say hey

00:19:29 --> 00:19:33
we're going to go to war with a country

00:19:31 --> 00:19:34
but by the way there's going to be no

00:19:32 --> 00:19:35
death

00:19:33 --> 00:19:37
right okay like that doesn't sound

00:19:34 --> 00:19:39
nearly as bad as hey we're going to go

00:19:36 --> 00:19:40
send 10 000 soldiers and you know some

00:19:38 --> 00:19:41
percentage of them are gonna die on the

00:19:39 --> 00:19:43
battlefield and then we're gonna

00:19:40 --> 00:19:44
you know basically pipe back videos and

00:19:42 --> 00:19:46
articles

00:19:43 --> 00:19:48
aying that american soldiers are dying

00:19:45 --> 00:19:49
yeah it does seem to be a fundamental

00:19:47 --> 00:19:53
difference between the

00:19:48 --> 00:19:53
genghis khan style like if i would

00:19:52 --> 00:19:57
feel

00:19:52 --> 00:20:00
differently if somebody

00:19:56 --> 00:20:01
like hand to hand with a knife murder my

00:19:59 --> 00:20:04
family

00:20:00 --> 00:20:05
versus cyber security where i stole all

00:20:03 --> 00:20:07
their data

00:20:04 --> 00:20:08
stole all their money stole everything

00:20:06 --> 00:20:10
they owned

00:20:07 --> 00:20:11
falsified their identity all that kind

00:20:09 --> 00:20:14
of stuff that

00:20:10 --> 00:20:17
hose are both traumatic events

00:20:13 --> 00:20:18
but they do seem to be fundamentally

00:20:16 --> 00:20:21
different but maybe that's

00:20:17 --> 00:20:22
actually very narrow style thinking

00:20:20 --> 00:20:24
because

00:20:21 --> 00:20:26
ultimately you have to think about what

00:20:23 --> 00:20:30
is life and what's happiness

00:20:25 --> 00:20:34
and it's like the samurai thinking

00:20:29 --> 00:20:36
i'm not sure what's more painful if i

00:20:33 --> 00:20:37
take it to myself like i'm not sure what

00:20:35 --> 00:20:40
i would rather

00:20:36 --> 00:20:44
live through being stabbed

00:20:39 --> 00:20:47
like to death or

00:20:43 --> 00:20:51
having my identity stolen all my

00:20:46 --> 00:20:51
money stolen or maybe

00:20:50 --> 00:20:56
reputation destroyed like with lies or

00:20:53 --> 00:20:57
something like that

00:20:55 --> 00:20:59
that's very interesting to think

00:20:56 --> 00:21:00
about if you think about quality of life

00:20:58 --> 00:21:04
and all those kinds of things

00:20:59 --> 00:21:06
well one that's finite right

00:21:03 --> 00:21:07
yeah and the other is kind of a long

00:21:05 --> 00:21:09
prolonged almost torture

00:21:06 --> 00:21:11
physical pain versus psychological pain

00:21:08 --> 00:21:13
uh it's really interesting to

00:21:10 --> 00:21:15
think about

00:21:12 --> 00:21:16
and i think another key piece to this um

00:21:14 --> 00:21:18
which i don't have answers to

00:21:16 --> 00:21:21
but but there is an element of emotion

00:21:17 --> 00:21:23
and rage in the physical violence versus

00:21:20 --> 00:21:25
again more of that clinical you know

00:21:22 --> 00:21:28
information warfare and so

00:21:24 --> 00:21:31
um it's really easy to show a

00:21:27 --> 00:21:32
battlefield where there's death on both

00:21:30 --> 00:21:33
sides and
